| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[characters/othello\|Otello]], [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]], Lodovico, [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]] e i servitori |
| LODOVICO | Vi supplico, signore, non incomodatevi oltre. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTELLO]] | Oh, perdonatemi, mi farà bene camminare. |
| LODOVICO | (a [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]])<br>Madonna, buona [[motifs/night\|notte]]. Ringrazio umilmente vostra signoria. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Il vostro [[concepts/honour\|onore]] è il benvenuto. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTELLO]] | Volete camminare, signore?<br>Oh, [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]]! |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Mio signore? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTELLO]] | Andate a letto sull'istante. Sarò di ritorno immediatamente. Congedate la vostra cameriera che è là. Badate che sia fatto. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Lo farò, mio signore. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono [[characters/othello\|Otello]], Lodovico e i servitori |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Come va ora? Ha l'aria più mite di prima. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Dice che tornerà immantinente.<br>Mi ha comandato d'andare a letto,<br>e mi ha ordinato di congedarti. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Congedarmi? |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Fu suo ordine. Perciò, buona [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]],<br>dammi la mia veste da notte, e addio. is<br>Non dobbiamo ora dispiacergli. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Vorrei che non l'aveste mai veduto. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Io no. Il mio [[motifs/love\|amore]] tanto l'approva<br>che perfino la sua ostinazione, i suoi rimproveri, i suoi cipigli—<br>ti prego, sfibbiami—hanno in sé grazia e favore.<br>[[characters/emilia\|Emilia]] aiuta [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]] a svestirsi |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Ho steso sul letto quelle lenzuola che m'avete chiesto. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Fa lo stesso. In buona [[concepts/faith\|fede]], come sono sciocche le nostre menti!<br>Se io morissi prima di te, ti prego, avvolgimi<br>in una di queste stesse lenzuola. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Suvvia, suvvia, voi parlate a vanvera. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Mia madre aveva una serva che si chiamava Barbara.<br>Era [[motifs/love\|innamorata]], e colui che amava impazzì<br>e l'abbandonò. Ella aveva una canzone del salice.<br>Era una cosa antica, ma esprimeva la sua sorte,<br>ed ella morì cantandola. Quella canzone stanotte<br>non mi vuole uscire di mente. Ho un gran daffare<br>a non andare a reclinare il capo tutto da un lato<br>e cantarla, come la povera Barbara. Ti prego, sbrigati. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Devo andare a prendere la vostra veste da notte? |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | No. Sfibbiami qui.<br>Questo Lodovico è un bell'uomo. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Un uomo molto avvenente. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Parla [[motifs/well\|bene]]. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Conosco una dama a [[settings/venice\|Venezia]] che avrebbe camminato scalza fino in Palestina per un tocco del suo labbro inferiore. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | (canta)<br>‘La povera [[motifs/soul\|anima]] sedeva sospirando sotto un sicomoro,<br>cantate tutti il verde salice.<br>La mano sul petto, il capo sul ginocchio,<br>cantate salice, salice, salice.<br>I freschi ruscelli le correvano accanto e mormoravano i suoi<br>lamenti,<br>cantate salice, salice, salice.<br>Le sue salate [[motifs/tears\|lacrime]] cadevano da lei e ammollivano le pietre,<br>cantate salice’—<br>Riponi questi.—<br>‘salice, salice.’<br>Ti prego, affrettati. Verrà tra poco.<br>‘Cantate tutti il verde salice sia la mia ghirlanda.<br>‘Nessuno lo biasimi, il suo disprezzo io l'approvo’—<br>No, non è questo che segue. Ascolta, chi è che bussa? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | È il [[motifs/wind\|vento]]. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | (canta)<br>‘Chiamai il mio [[motifs/love\|amore]] falso [[motifs/love\|amore]], ma egli che disse allora?<br>cantate salice, salice, salice.<br>Se io [[settings/court\|corteggio]] più donne, tu giacerai con più uomini.’<br>Dunque, vattene. Buona [[motifs/night\|notte]]. Mi prudono gli occhi. 56<br>Presagisce ciò un pianto? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Non significa né l'uno né l'altro. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | L'ho sentito dire. Oh, questi uomini, questi uomini!<br>Credi tu in [[concepts/conscience\|coscienza]]—dimmi, [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]]—<br>che vi siano donne che ingannano i loro mariti<br>in modo così grossolano?<br>EMILIACe ne sono di tali, non c'è dubbio. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Faresti tu una tale azione per tutto il mondo? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Come, voi non la fareste? |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | No, per questa luce celeste. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Neppure io, per questa luce celeste. Potrei farlo<br>altrettanto [[motifs/well\|bene]] al buio. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Faresti tu una tale azione per tutto il mondo? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Il mondo è cosa immensa. È un gran prezzo<br>per un piccolo vizio. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | In [[concepts/truth\|verità]], credo che tu non lo faresti. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | In [[concepts/truth\|verità]], credo che lo farei, e lo disfarei dopo averlo fatto. Certo, non farei una cosa simile per un anello a incastro, né per pezze di batista, né per vesti, sottane, né cuffie, né alcun meschino donativo; ma per tutto il mondo intero? Per la pietà di Dio, chi non farebbe becco il proprio marito per farlo monarca? Rischierei il purgatorio per questo. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Ch'io sia maledetta se facessi un tal torto<br>per il mondo intero. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Ma il torto non è che un torto nel mondo, e avendo il mondo per vostra fatica, è un torto nel vostro proprio mondo, e potreste presto raddrizzarlo. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Non credo che esista una donna simile. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Sì, una dozzina, e altrettante<br>in soprappiù da popolare il mondo per cui<br>giocarono.<br>Ma io credo che sia colpa dei loro mariti<br>se le mogli cadono. Poniamo che trascurino i loro doveri,<br>e versino i nostri tesori in grembi stranieri,<br>oppure scoppino in stizzose gelosie,<br>imponendoci restrizioni; o poniamo che ci battano,<br>o riducano per dispetto quanto prima ci davano:<br>ebbene, noi abbiamo fiele; e sebbene abbiamo qualche grazia,<br>pure abbiamo qualche [[concepts/revenge\|vendetta]]. Sappiano i mariti<br>che le loro mogli hanno sensi come loro. Vedono, e odorano,<br>e hanno palato tanto per il dolce quanto per l'aspro,<br>come l'hanno i mariti. Che cos'è che fanno<br>quando ci cambiano con altre? È diporto?<br>Io credo di sì. E l'affetto lo genera?<br>Credo di sì. È fragilità che così erra?<br>Anche questo è vero. E non abbiamo noi affetti,<br>desideri di diporto, e fragilità, come gli uomini?<br>Allora ci trattino [[motifs/well\|bene]], o sappiano<br>che i mali che facciamo, i loro mali ce li insegnano. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Buona [[motifs/night\|notte]], buona [[motifs/night\|notte]]. Dio mi mandi tali costumi<br>non di trarre il male dal male, ma col male emendarmi! Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_5--scene_1" data-title="Othello — Act V, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/villain,motif/blood,motif/death,motif/home,motif/night,motif/well,concept/beauty,concept/body,concept/fear,concept/mercy,setting/venice,character/bianca,character/cassio,character/desdemona,character/emilia,character/iago,character/othello,character/roderigo"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/iago\|Iago]] and [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]] |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Here, stand behind this bulk. Straight will he come.<br>Wear thy good rapier bare, and put it [[motifs/home\|home]].<br>Quick, quick, [[concepts/fear\|fear]] nothing. I’ll be at thy elbow.<br>It makes us or it mars us. Think on that,<br>And fix most firm thy resolution. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | Be near at hand. I may miscarry in’t. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Here at thy hand. Be bold, and take thy stand. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | (aside)<br>I have no great devotion to the deed,<br>And yet he hath given me satisfying reasons.<br>’Tis but a man gone. Forth my sword—he dies! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (aside)<br>I have rubbed this young quat almost to the sense,<br>And he grows angry. Now, whether he kill [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]<br>Or [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] him, or each do kill the other,<br>Every way makes my gain. live [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]],<br>He calls me to a restitution large<br>Of gold and jewels that I bobbed from him<br>As gifts to [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]].<br>It must not be. If [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] do remain,<br>He hath a daily [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]] in his life<br>That makes me ugly; and besides, the Moor<br>May unfold me to him-there stand I in much peril.<br>No, he must die. But so, I hear him coming. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | I know his gait, ’tis he. (Attacking [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]) [[archetypes/villain\|Villain]], thou<br>diest. |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | That thrust had been mine enemy indeed,<br>But that my coat is better than thou know’st.<br>I will make proof of thine.<br>He stabs [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]], who falls |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | O, I am slain!<br>[[characters/iago\|Iago]] wounds [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] in the leg from behind. Exit [[characters/iago\|Iago]] |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | (falling)<br>I am maimed for ever. Help, ho, murder, murder! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/othello\|Othello]] [above] |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | The voice of [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]. [[characters/iago\|Iago]] keeps his word. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | O, [[archetypes/villain\|villain]] that I am! |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | It is even so. |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | O, help, ho! Light, a surgeon! |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | ’Tis he. O brave [[characters/iago\|Iago]], honest and just,<br>That hast such noble sense of thy friend’s wrong—<br>Thou teachest me. Minion, your dear lies dead,<br>And your unblessed fate hies. Strumpet, I come.<br>Forth of my heart those charms, thine eyes, are blotted.<br>Thy bed, lust-stained, shall with lust’s [[motifs/blood\|blood]] be spotted. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lodovico and Graziano |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | What ho, no watch, no passage? Murder, murder! |
| GRAZIANO | ’Tis some mischance. The voice is very direful. |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | O, help! |
| LODOVICO | Hark. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | O wretched [[archetypes/villain\|villain]]! |
| LODOVICO | Two or three groan. ’Tis heavy [[motifs/night\|night]].<br>These may be counterfeits. Let’s think’t unsafe<br>To come into the cry without more help. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | Nobody come? Then shall I bleed to [[motifs/death\|death]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/iago\|Iago]] with a light |
| LODOVICO | Hark. |
| GRAZIANO | Here’s one comes in his shirt, with light and weapons. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Who’s there? Whose noise is this that cries on murder? |
| LODOVICO | We do not know. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Do not you hear a cry? |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | Here, here. For heaven’s sake, help me. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | What’s the matter? |
| GRAZIANO | (to Lodovico)<br>This is [[characters/othello\|Othello]]’s ensign, as I take it. |
| LODOVICO | The same indeed, a very valiant fellow. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (to [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]])<br>What are you here that cry so grievously? |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | [[characters/iago\|Iago]]—O, I am spoiled, undone by villains.<br>Give me some help. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | O me, lieutenant, what villains have done this? |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | I think that one of them is hereabout<br>And cannot make away. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | O treacherous villains!<br>(To Lodovico and Graziano)<br>What are you there? Come in and give some help. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | O, help me there! |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | That’s one of ’em. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (stabbing [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]) O murderous slave! O [[archetypes/villain\|villain]]! |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | O damned [[characters/iago\|Iago]]! O inhuman dog! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Kill men i’th’ dark? Where be these bloody thieves?<br>How silent is this town! Ho, murder, murder!<br>(To Lodovico and Graziano)<br>What may you be? Are you of good or evil? |
| LODOVICO | As you shall prove us, praise us. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Signor Lodovico. |
| LODOVICO | He, sir. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | I cry you [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]]. Here’s [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] hurt by villains. |
| GRAZIANO | [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]? |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | How is’t, brother? |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | My leg is cut in two. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Marry, heaven forbid!<br>Light, gentlemen. I’ll bind it with my shirt. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]] |
| [[characters/bianca\|BIANCA]] | What is the matter, ho? Who is’t that cried? |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Who is’t that cried? |
| [[characters/bianca\|BIANCA]] | O my dear [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]],<br>My sweet [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], O, [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | O notable strumpet! [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], may you suspect<br>Who they should be that have thus mangled you? 80 |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | No. |
| GRAZIANO | I am sorry to find you thus. I have been to seek you. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Lend me a garter. So. O for a chair,<br>To bear him easily hence! |
| [[characters/bianca\|BIANCA]] | Alas, he faints. O, [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Gentlemen all, I do suspect this trash<br>To be a party in this injury.<br>Patience a while, good [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]. Come, come,<br>Lend me a light. (Going to [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]) Know we this face<br>or no?<br>Alas, my friend, and my dear countryman.<br>[[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]? No—yes, sure—O heaven, [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]! |
| GRAZIANO | What, of [[settings/venice\|Venice]]? |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Even he, sir. Did you know him? |
| GRAZIANO | Know him? Ay. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Signor Graziano, I cry your gentle pardon.<br>These bloody accidents must excuse my manners<br>That so neglected you. |
| GRAZIANO | I am glad to see you. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | How do you, [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]? O, a chair, a chair! |
| GRAZIANO | [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | He, he, ’tis he. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter attendants with a chair |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | O, that’s [[motifs/well\|well]] said, the chair!<br>Some good man bear him carefully from hence.<br>I’ll fetch the general’s surgeon. (To [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) For you,<br>mistress,<br>Save you your labour. He that lies slain here, [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]],<br>Was my dear friend. What malice was between you? |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | None in the world, nor do I know the man. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (to [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]])<br>What, look you pale? (To attendants) O, bear him out<br>o’th’ air.<br>(To Lodovico and Graziano)<br>Stay you, good gentlemen. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt attendants with [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] in the chair [and with [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]’s [[concepts/body\|body]]] |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (To [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) Look you pale, mistress?<br>(To Lodovico and Graziano)<br>Do you perceive the ghastness of her eye?<br>(To [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) Nay, an you stare we shall hear more<br>anon.<br>(To Lodovico and Graziano)<br>Behold her [[motifs/well\|well]]; I pray you look upon her.<br>Do you see, gentlemen? Nay, guiltiness<br>Will speak, though tongues were out of use. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]] |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Alas, what is the matter? What is the matter,<br>husband? |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] hath here been set on in the dark<br>By [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]] and fellows that are scaped.<br>He’s almost slain, and [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]] dead. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Alas, good gentleman! Alas, good [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | This is the fruits of whoring. Prithee, [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]],<br>Go know of [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] where he supped tonight.<br>(To [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) What, do you shake at that? |
| [[characters/bianca\|BIANCA]] | He supped at my house, but I therefore shake not. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | O, did he so? I charge you go with me. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | (to [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) O, fie upon thee, strumpet! |
| [[characters/bianca\|BIANCA]] | I am no strumpet, but of life as honest<br>As you that thus abuse me. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | As I? Fough, fie upon thee! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Kind gentlemen, let’s go see poor [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] dressed.<br>(To [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) Come, mistress, you must tell’s another tale.<br>[[characters/emilia\|Emilia]], run you to the citadel<br>And tell my lord and lady what hath happed.<br>Will you go on afore? |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]] |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (Aside) This is the [[motifs/night\|night]]<br>That either makes me or fordoes me quite. Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[characters/iago\|Iago]] e [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]] |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Qui, stai dietro questo sporto. Verrà subito.<br>Tieni sguainato il tuo buon stocco, e vibralo a [[motifs/home\|fondo]].<br>Presto, presto, non [[concepts/fear\|temere]] nulla. Sarò al tuo fianco.<br>Ci fa o ci disfa. Pensa a questo,<br>e rendi fermissima la tua risoluzione. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | Stammi vicino. Potrei fallire nell’impresa. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Qui alla tua mano. Sii ardito, e prendi posizione. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | (a parte)<br>Non ho gran devozione per l’impresa,<br>eppure egli m’ha dato ragioni convincenti.<br>Non è che un uomo di meno. Fuori la mia spada—muore! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (a parte)<br>Ho stropicciato questo giovane foruncolo quasi a vivo,<br>ed egli s’adira. Ora, che egli uccida [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]<br>o [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] lui, o che ciascuno uccida l’altro,<br>in ogni modo è mio guadagno. viva [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]],<br>egli mi richiama a una larga restituzione<br>d’oro e di gioielli che gli ho carpito<br>come doni per [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]].<br>Non dev’essere. Se [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] resta in vita,<br>egli ha nella sua vita una quotidiana [[concepts/beauty\|bellezza]]<br>che mi fa brutto; e inoltre, il Moro<br>potrebbe svelarmi a lui-là io sto in gran pericolo.<br>No, deve morire. Ma ecco, lo sento venire. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | Conosco il suo passo, è lui. (Attaccando [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]) [[archetypes/villain\|Furfante]], tu<br>muori. |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | Quel colpo sarebbe stato davvero mio nemico,<br>se non fosse che la mia giubba è migliore di quel che credi.<br>Farò prova della tua.<br>Pugnala [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]], che cade |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | Oh, sono ucciso!<br>[[characters/iago\|Iago]] ferisce [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] alla gamba da dietro. Esce [[characters/iago\|Iago]] |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | (cadendo)<br>Sono storpiato per sempre. Aiuto, olà, assassinio, assassinio! |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[characters/othello\|Otello]] [in alto] |
| [[characters/othello\|OTELLO]] | La voce di [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]. [[characters/iago\|Iago]] mantiene la parola. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | Oh, [[archetypes/villain\|furfante]] che sono! |
| [[characters/othello\|OTELLO]] | È proprio così. |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | Oh, aiuto, olà! Un lume, un cerusico! |
| [[characters/othello\|OTELLO]] | È lui. Oh prode [[characters/iago\|Iago]], onesto e giusto,<br>che hai così nobile sentimento del torto fatto al tuo amico—<br>tu mi ammaestri. Bamboccia, il tuo caro giace morto,<br>e il tuo maledetto destino s’affretta. Sgualdrina, io vengo.<br>Dal mio cuore quegl’incanti, i tuoi occhi, sono cancellati.<br>Il tuo letto, macchiato di lussuria, sarà chiazzato del [[motifs/blood\|sangue]] della lussuria. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano Lodovico e Graziano |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | Olà, nessuna guardia, nessun passante? Assassinio, assassinio! |
| GRAZIANO | È qualche sciagura. La voce è assai spaventosa. |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | Oh, aiuto! |
| LODOVICO | Ascoltate. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | Oh miserabile [[archetypes/villain\|furfante]]! |
| LODOVICO | Due o tre gemono. È [[motifs/night\|notte]] fonda.<br>Potrebbero esser finzioni. Reputiamo malsicuro<br>accorrere al grido senza maggior aiuto. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | Nessuno viene? Allora mi dissanguerò a [[motifs/death\|morte]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[characters/iago\|Iago]] con un lume |
| LODOVICO | Ascoltate. |
| GRAZIANO | Eccone uno che viene in camicia, con lume e armi. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Chi è là? Di chi è questo strepito che grida all’assassinio? |
| LODOVICO | Non lo sappiamo. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Non udite un grido? |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | Qui, qui. Per amor del cielo, aiutatemi. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Che c’è? |
| GRAZIANO | (a Lodovico)<br>Questo è l’alfiere di [[characters/othello\|Otello]], se non erro. |
| LODOVICO | Proprio lui, un uomo assai valoroso. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (a [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]])<br>Chi siete voi qui che gridate così dolorosamente? |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | [[characters/iago\|Iago]]—oh, sono rovinato, disfatto da furfanti.<br>Datemi aiuto. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Ohimè, luogotenente, quali furfanti han fatto questo? |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | Credo che uno di loro sia qui intorno<br>e non possa fuggire. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Oh furfanti traditori!<br>(A Lodovico e Graziano)<br>Chi siete voi là? Venite qua e date aiuto. |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | Oh, aiutatemi qua! |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | Quello è uno di loro. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (pugnalando [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]) Oh schiavo assassino! Oh [[archetypes/villain\|furfante]]! |
| [[characters/roderigo\|RODERIGO]] | Oh dannato [[characters/iago\|Iago]]! Oh cane disumano! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Uccidere gli uomini al buio? Dove sono questi ladri sanguinari?<br>Com’è silenziosa questa città! Olà, assassinio, assassinio!<br>(A Lodovico e Graziano)<br>Chi potreste essere? Siete del bene o del male? |
| LODOVICO | Come ci proverete, così lodateci. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Signor Lodovico. |
| LODOVICO | Lui, signore. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Vi chiedo [[concepts/mercy\|perdono]]. Ecco [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] ferito da furfanti. |
| GRAZIANO | [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]? |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Come va, fratello? |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | La mia gamba è tagliata in due. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Perbacco, il cielo non voglia!<br>Un lume, signori. La fascerò con la mia camicia. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]] |
| [[characters/bianca\|BIANCA]] | Che c’è, olà? Chi è che ha gridato? |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Chi è che ha gridato? |
| [[characters/bianca\|BIANCA]] | Oh mio caro [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]],<br>mio dolce [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], oh, [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Oh insigne sgualdrina! [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], potete sospettare<br>chi possano essere coloro che così vi hanno straziato? 80 |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | No. |
| GRAZIANO | Mi duole trovarvi così. Sono andato a cercarvi. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Prestatemi una giarrettiera. Così. Oh, una sedia,<br>per portarlo via di qua agevolmente! |
| [[characters/bianca\|BIANCA]] | Ahimè, sviene. Oh, [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Signori tutti, sospetto che questa spazzatura<br>sia parte in questo oltraggio.<br>Pazienza per un poco, buon [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]. Venite, venite,<br>prestatemi un lume. (Andando verso [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]) Conosciamo questo volto<br>o no?<br>Ahimè, il mio amico, e mio caro compatriota.<br>[[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]? No—sì, certo—oh cielo, [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]! |
| GRAZIANO | Che, quello di [[settings/venice\|Venezia]]? |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Proprio lui, signore. Lo conoscevate? |
| GRAZIANO | Conoscerlo? Sì. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Signor Graziano, imploro il vostro gentile perdono.<br>Questi sanguinosi accidenti scusino i miei modi<br>che così vi trascurarono. |
| GRAZIANO | Sono lieto di vedervi. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Come state, [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]? Oh, una sedia, una sedia! |
| GRAZIANO | [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Lui, lui, è lui. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano servitori con una sedia |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Oh, questo è [[motifs/well\|ben]] detto, la sedia!<br>Qualche buon uomo lo porti via di qua con cura.<br>Andrò a chiamare il cerusico del generale. (A [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) Quanto a voi,<br>padrona,<br>risparmiate la vostra fatica. Colui che giace qui ucciso, [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]],<br>era mio caro amico. Che rancore c’era fra voi? |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | Nessuno al mondo, né conosco quell’uomo. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (a [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]])<br>Che, impallidite? (Ai servitori) Oh, portatelo via<br>dall’aria aperta.<br>(A Lodovico e Graziano)<br>Restate voi, buoni signori. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono i servitori con [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] nella sedia [e con il [[concepts/body\|corpo]] di [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]] |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (A [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) Impallidite, padrona?<br>(A Lodovico e Graziano)<br>Notate lo spavento del suo occhio?<br>(A [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) Suvvia, se sbarrate gli occhi udremo altro<br>fra poco.<br>(A Lodovico e Graziano)<br>Osservatela [[motifs/well\|bene]]; vi prego, guardatela.<br>Vedete, signori? Suvvia, la colpa<br>parlerà, anche se le lingue fossero fuori uso. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]] |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Ahimè, che cosa succede? Che cosa succede,<br>marito? |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] è stato qui assalito nel buio<br>da [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]] e da compari che sono scampati.<br>È quasi ucciso, e [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]] è morto. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Ahimè, buon gentiluomo! Ahimè, buon [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Questi sono i frutti del puttaneggiare. Ti prego, [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]],<br>va’ a sapere da [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] dove ha cenato stanotte.<br>(A [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) Che, tremate a questo? |
| [[characters/bianca\|BIANCA]] | Ha cenato a casa mia, ma non per questo tremo. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Oh, davvero? Vi ingiungo di venire con me. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | (a [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) Oh, vergogna su di te, sgualdrina! |
| [[characters/bianca\|BIANCA]] | Non sono una sgualdrina, ma di vita onesta<br>quanto voi che così m’oltraggiate. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Quanto me? Puah, vergogna su di te! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Gentili signori, andiamo a veder medicare il povero [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]].<br>(A [[characters/bianca\|Bianca]]) Venite, padrona, dovrete raccontarci un’altra storia.<br>[[characters/emilia\|Emilia]], corri tu alla cittadella<br>e di’ al mio signore e alla mia signora ciò che è accaduto.<br>Volete andare avanti? |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]] |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | (A parte) Questa è la [[motifs/night\|notte]]<br>che o mi fa o mi disfa del tutto. Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_5--scene_2" data-title="Othello — Act V, Scene 2" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/fool,archetype/innocent,archetype/king,archetype/villain,motif/blood,motif/death,motif/desire,motif/fire,motif/grave,motif/home,motif/journey,motif/love,motif/mirror,motif/moon,motif/nature,motif/pain,motif/prayer,motif/rose,motif/snow,motif/soul,motif/stars,motif/storm,motif/tears,motif/time,motif/water,motif/well,concept/body,concept/fear,concept/history,concept/honour,concept/hope,concept/madness,concept/mercy,concept/nature,concept/revenge,concept/truth,form/fable,form/history,setting/oxford,character/cassio,character/cordelia,character/cressida,character/desdemona,character/edgar,character/edmund,character/emilia,character/gloucester,character/hamlet,character/iago,character/kent,character/king_lear,character/othello,character/regan,character/roderigo,character/troilus"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/othello\|Othello]] with a light. He draws back a curtain, revealing [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]] asleep in her bed |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | It is the cause, it is the cause, my [[motifs/soul\|soul]].<br>Let me not name it to you, you chaste [[motifs/stars\|stars]].<br>It is the cause. Yet I’ll not shed her [[motifs/blood\|blood]],<br>Nor scar that whiter skin of hers than [[motifs/snow\|snow]],<br>And smooth as monumental alabaster.<br>Yet she must die, else she’ll betray more men.<br>Put out the light, and then put out the light.<br>If I quench thee, thou flaming minister,<br>I can again thy former light restore<br>Should I repent me; but once put out thy light,<br>Thou cunning‘st pattern of excelling [[concepts/nature\|nature]],<br>I know not where is that Promethean heat<br>That can thy light relume. When I have plucked thy<br>[[motifs/rose\|rose]]<br>I cannot give it vital growth again.<br>It needs must wither. I’ll smell thee on the tree.<br>[He kisses her]<br>O balmy breath, that dost almost persuade<br>Justice to break her sword! One more, one more.<br>Be thus when thou art dead, and I will kill thee<br>And [[motifs/love\|love]] thee after. One more, and that’s the last.<br>He kisses her<br>So sweet was ne’er so fatal. I must weep,<br>But they are cruel [[motifs/tears\|tears]]. This sorrow’s heavenly,<br>It strikes where it doth [[motifs/love\|love]]. She wakes. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Who’s there? [[characters/othello\|Othello]]? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Ay, [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]]. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Will you come to bed, my lord?<br>OTHELLOHave you prayed tonight, Desdemon? |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Ay, my lord. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | If you bethink yourself of any crime<br>Unreconciled as yet to heaven and grace,<br>Solicit for it straight. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Alack, my lord, what may you mean by that? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], do it, and be brief. I will walk by.<br>I would not kill thy unprepared spirit.<br>No, heavens forfend! I would not kill thy [[motifs/soul\|soul]]. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Talk you of killing? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Ay, I do. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Then heaven<br>Have [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] on me. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Amen, with all my heart. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | If you say so, I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] you will not kill me. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Hum! |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | And yet I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] you, for you’re fatal then<br>When your eyes roll so. Why I should [[concepts/fear\|fear]] I know not,<br>Since guiltiness I know not, but yet I feel I [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Think on thy sins. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | They are loves I bear to you. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Ay, and for that thou diest. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | That [[motifs/death\|death]]’s unnatural that kills for loving.<br>Alas, why gnaw you so your nether lip?<br>Some bloody passion shakes your very frame.<br>These are portents, but yet I [[concepts/hope\|hope]], I [[concepts/hope\|hope]]<br>They do not point on me. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Peace, and be still. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | I will so. What’s the matter? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | That handkerchief<br>Which I so loved and gave thee, thou gav’st to [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | No, by my life and [[motifs/soul\|soul]]. Send for the man<br>And ask him. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Sweet [[motifs/soul\|soul]], take heed, take heed of perjury.<br>Thou art on thy deathbed. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Ay, but not yet to die. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Presently.<br>Therefore confess thee freely of thy sin,<br>For to deny each article with oath<br>Cannot remove nor choke the strong conception<br>That I do groan withal. Thou art to die. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Then Lord have [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] on me. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | I say amen. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | And have you [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]], too. I never did<br>Offend you in my life, never loved [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]<br>But with such general warranty of heaven<br>As I might [[motifs/love\|love]]. I never gave him token. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | By heaven, I saw my handkerchief in’s hand.<br>O perjured woman! Thou dost stone my heart,<br>And makes me call what I intend to do<br>A murder, which I thought a sacrifice.<br>I saw the handkerchief. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | He found it, then.<br>I never gave it him. Send for him hither.<br>Let him confess a [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | He hath confessed. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | What, my lord? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | That he hath used thee. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | How, unlawfully? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Ay. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | He will not say so.<br>OTHELLONo, his mouth is stopped.<br>Honest [[characters/iago\|Iago]] hath ta’en order for’t. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | O, my [[concepts/fear\|fear]] interprets! What, is he dead? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Had all his hairs been lives, my great [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]]<br>Had stomach for em all. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | (weeping)<br>Alas, he is betrayed, and I undone. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Out, strumpet! Weep’st thou for him to my face? |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | O, banish me, my lord, but kill me not. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | (attacking her) Down, strumpet. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Kill me tomorrow; let me live tonight. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Nay, an you strive— |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | But half an hour. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Being done, there is no pause. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | But while I say one [[motifs/prayer\|prayer]]. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | It is too late.<br>He smothers her |
| DESDEMONAO | Lord, Lord, Lord!<br>[[characters/emilia\|Emilia]] at the door |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | (within)<br>My lord, my lord, what ho, my lord, my lord! |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | What noise is this? Not dead? Not yet quite dead?<br>I that am cruel am yet merciful.<br>I would not have thee linger in thy [[motifs/pain\|pain]].<br>So, so. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | (within) What ho, my lord, my lord!<br>OTHELLOWho’s there? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | (within)<br>O, good my lord, I would speak a word with you. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | (calling)<br>Yes.—‘Tis [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]]. (Calling) By and by!—She’s dead.<br>’Tis like she comes to speak of [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]’s [[motifs/death\|death]].<br>The noise was high. Ha! No more moving.<br>Still as the [[motifs/grave\|grave]]. Shall she come in? Were’t good?<br>I think she stirs again. No. What’s best to do?<br>If she come in, she’ll sure speak to my wife.<br>My wife, my wife! What wife? I ha’ no wife.<br>O insupportable, O heavy hour!<br>Methinks it should be now a huge eclipse<br>Of sun and [[motifs/moon\|moon]], and that th’affrighted globe<br>Should yawn at alteration. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | (within) I do beseech you<br>That I may speak with you. O, good my lord! |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | I had forgot thee.—O, come in, [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]].—<br>Soft, by and by. Let me the curtains draw.<br>He closes the bed-curtains<br>Where art thou?<br>He opens the door. Enter [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]]<br>What’s the matter with thee now? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | O my good lord, yonder’s foul murder’s done. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | What, now? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | But now, my lord. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | It is the very error of the [[motifs/moon\|moon]],<br>She comes more nearer earth than she was wont,<br>And makes men mad. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], my lord, hath killed a young Venetian<br>Called [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]] killed<br>And [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] killed? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | No, [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] is not killed. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Not [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] killed? Then murder’s out of tune,<br>And sweet [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]] grows harsh. |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | O, falsely, falsely murdered! |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | O Lord, what cry is that? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | That? What? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Out and alas, that was my lady’s voice! |
| *(didascalia)* | She opens the bed-curtains |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Help, help, ho, help ! O lady, speak again!<br>Sweet [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]], O sweet mistress, speak! |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | A guiltless [[motifs/death\|death]] I die. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | O, who hath done this deed? |
| [[characters/desdemona\|DESDEMONA]] | Nobody, I myself. Farewell.<br>Commend me to my kind lord. O, farewell! She dies |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Why, how should she be murdered? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Alas, who knows? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | You heard her say herself it was not I. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | She said so, I must needs report the [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | She’s like a liar gone to burning hell.<br>’Twas I that killed her. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | O, the more angel she, and you the blacker devil! |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | She turned to folly, and she was a whore. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Thou dost belie her, and thou art a devil. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | She was false as [[motifs/water\|water]]. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Thou art rash as [[motifs/fire\|fire]]<br>To say that she was false. O, she was heavenly true! |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] did top her. Ask thy husband else.<br>O, I were damned beneath all depth in hell<br>But that I did proceed upon just grounds<br>To this extremity! Thy husband knew it all. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | My husband? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Thy husband. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | That she was false to wedlock? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Ay, with [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]. Had she been true,<br>If heaven would make me such another world<br>Of one entire and perfect chrysolite<br>I’d not have sold her for it. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | My husband? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Ay, ’twas he that told me on her first.<br>An honest man he is, and hates the slime<br>That sticks on filthy deeds. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | My husband? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | What needs this iterance? Woman, I say thy husband. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | O mistress, villainy hath made mocks with [[motifs/love\|love]].<br>My husband say she was false? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | He, woman.<br>I say thy husband. Dost understand the word?<br>My friend, thy husband, honest, honest [[characters/iago\|Iago]]. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | If he say so, may his pernicious [[motifs/soul\|soul]]<br>Rot half a grain a day. He lies to th’ heart.<br>She was too fond of her most filthy bargain. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Ha? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Do thy worst.<br>This deed of thine is no more worthy heaven<br>Than thou wast worthy her. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Peace, you were best. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Thou hast not half that power to do me harm<br>As I have to be hurt. O gull, O dolt,<br>As ignorant as dirt! Thou hast done a deed—<br>I care not for thy sword, I’ll make thee known<br>Though I lost twenty lives. Help, help, hot Help!<br>The Moor hath killed my mistress. Murder, murder! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Montano, Graziano, and [[characters/iago\|Iago]] |
| MONTANO | What is the matter? How now, general? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | O, are you come, [[characters/iago\|Iago]]? You have done [[motifs/well\|well]],<br>That men must lay their murders on your neck. |
| GRAZIANO | What is the matter? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | (to [[characters/iago\|Iago]])<br>Disprove this [[archetypes/villain\|villain]] if thou beest a man.<br>He says thou told‘st him that his wife was false.<br>I know thou didst not. Thou’rt not such a [[archetypes/villain\|villain]].<br>Speak, for my heart is full. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | I told him what I thought, and told no more<br>Than what he found himself was apt and true. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | But did you ever tell him she was false? |
| IACO | I did. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | You told a lie, an odious, damnèd lie,<br>Upon my [[motifs/soul\|soul]] a lie, a wicked lie.<br>She false with [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]? Did you say with [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]? |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | With [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], mistress. Go to, charm your tongue. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | I will not charm my tongue. I am bound to speak.<br>My mistress here lies murdered in her bed. |
| *(didascalia)* | MONTANO and GRAZIANO O heavens forfend! |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | (to [[characters/iago\|Iago]])<br>And your reports have set the murder on. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Nay, stare not, masters. It is true indeed. |
| GRAZIANO | ’Tis a strange [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. |
| MONTANO | O monstrous act! |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Villainy, villainy, villainy!<br>I think upon’t, I think. I smell’t. O villainy!<br>I thought so then. I’ll kill myself for grief.<br>O villainy, villainy! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | What, are you mad? I charge you get you [[motifs/home\|home]]. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Good gentlemen, let me have leave to speak.<br>‘Tis proper I obey him, but not now.<br>Perchance, [[characters/iago\|Iago]], I will ne’er go [[motifs/home\|home]]. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | O, O, O!<br>[[characters/othello\|Othello]] falls on the bed |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Nay, lay thee down and roar,<br>For thou hast killed the sweetest [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]]<br>That e’er did lift up eye. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] rising  O, she was foul! |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | (To Graziano) I scarce did know you, uncle. There lies<br>your niece,<br>Whose breath indeed these hands have newly<br>stopped.<br>I know this act shows horrible and grim |
| GRAZIANO | Poor Desdemon, I am glad thy father’s dead.<br>Thy match was mortal to him, and pure grief<br>Shore his old thread in twain. Did he live now<br>This sight would make him do a desperate turn,<br>Yea, curse his better angel from his side,<br>And fall to reprobance. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | ’Tis pitiful. But yet [[characters/iago\|Iago]] knows<br>That she with [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] hath the act of shame<br>A thousand times committed. [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] confessed it,<br>And she did gratify his amorous works<br>With that recognizance and pledge of [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>Which I first gave her. I saw it in his hand.<br>It was a handkerchief, an antique token<br>My father gave my mother. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | O God! O heavenly God! |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | ’Swounds, hold your peace! |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | ‘Twill out, ’twill out. I peace?<br>No, I will speak as liberal as the north.<br>Let heaven, and men, and devils, let ’em all,<br>All, all cry shame against me, yet I’ll speak. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Be wise and get you [[motifs/home\|home]]. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | I will not.<br>[[characters/iago\|Iago]] draws his sword |
| GRAZIANO | (to [[characters/iago\|Iago]]) Fie, your sword upon a woman? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | O thou dull Moor, that handkerchief thou speak’st of<br>I found by fortune and did give my husband,<br>For often, with a solemn earnestness—<br>More than indeed belonged to such a trifle—<br>He begged of me to steal’t. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Villainous whore! |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | She give it [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]? No, alas, I found it,<br>And I did give’t my husband. |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Filth, thou liest. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | By heaven I do not, I do not, gentlemen.<br>O murderous coxcomb! What should such a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]<br>Do with so good a wife? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Are there no stones in heaven<br>But what serves for the thunder? Precious [[archetypes/villain\|villain]]!<br>The Moor runs at lago. Montano disarms [[characters/othello\|Othello]]. [[characters/iago\|Iago]] wounds his wife |
| GRAZIANO | The woman falls. Sure he hath killed his wife. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Ay, ay. O, lay me by my mistress’ side! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit lago |
| GRAZIANO | He’s gone, but his wife’s killed. |
| MONTANO | ‘Tis a notorious [[archetypes/villain\|villain]]. Take you this weapon<br>Which I have here recovered from the Moor.<br>Come, guard the door without. Let him not pass,<br>But kill him rather. I’ll after that same [[archetypes/villain\|villain]],<br>For ’tis a damned slave. Exeunt Montano and Graziano |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | I am not valiant neither,<br>But every puny whipster gets my sword.<br>But why should [[concepts/honour\|honour]] outlive honesty?<br>Let it go all. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | What did thy song bode, lady?<br>Hark, canst thou hear me? I will play the swan,<br>And die in music. (Sings) ‘Willow, willow, willow.’—<br>Moor, she was chaste. She loved thee, cruel Moor.<br>So come my [[motifs/soul\|soul]] to bliss as I speak true.<br>So, speaking as I think, alas, I die. She dies |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | I have another weapon in this chamber.<br>It was a sword of Spain, the ice-brook’s temper.<br>O, here it is. (Calling) Uncle, I must come forth. |
| GRAZIANO | (within)<br>If thou attempt it, it will cost thee dear.<br>Thou hast no weapon, and perforce must suffer. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Look in upon me then, and speak with me,<br>Or, naked as I am, I will assault thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Graziano |
| GRAZIANO | What is the matter? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Behold, I have a weapon;<br>A better never did itself sustain<br>Upon a soldier’s thigh. I have seen the day<br>That, with this little arm and this good sword,<br>I have made my way through more impediments<br>Than twenty times your stop. But O, vain boast!<br>Who can control his fate? ‘Tis not so now.<br>Be not afraid, though you do see me weaponed.<br>Here is my [[motifs/journey\|journey]]’s end, here is my butt<br>And very sea-mark of my utmost sail.<br>Do you go back dismayed? ’Tis a lost [[concepts/fear\|fear]].<br>Man but a rush against [[characters/othello\|Othello]]’s breast<br>And he retires. Where should [[characters/othello\|Othello]] go?<br>(To [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]]) Now, how dost thou look now? O ill-<br>starred wench,<br>Pale as thy smock! When we shall meet at count<br>This look of thine will hurl my [[motifs/soul\|soul]] from heaven,<br>And fiends will snatch at it. |
| *(didascalia)* | He touches her |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Cold, cold, my girl,<br>Even like thy chastity. O cursed, cursed slavel<br>Whip me, ye devils,<br>From the possession of this heavenly sight.<br>Blow me about in winds, roast me in sulphur,<br>Wash me in steep-down gulfs of liquid [[motifs/fire\|fire]]!<br>O Desdemon! Dead Desdemon! Dead! 0! 0! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lodovico, Montano, [[characters/iago\|Iago]] guarded by officers, with [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] in a chair |
| LODOVICO | Where is this rash and most unfortunate man? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | That’s he that was [[characters/othello\|Othello]]. Here I am. |
| LODOVICO | Where is that viper? Bring the [[archetypes/villain\|villain]] forth. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | I look down towards his feet, but that’s a [[forms/fable\|fable]].<br>(To [[characters/iago\|Iago]]) If that thou beest a devil I cannot kill thee.<br>He wounds [[characters/iago\|Iago]] |
| LODOVICO | Wrench his sword from him.<br>Officers disarm [[characters/othello\|Othello]] |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | I bleed, sir, but not killed. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | I am not sorry, neither. I’d have thee live,<br>For in my sense ’tis happiness to die. |
| LODOVICO | O thou [[characters/othello\|Othello]], that was once so good,<br>Fall’n in the practice of a cursed slave,<br>What shall be said to thee? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Why, anything;<br>An honourable murderer, if you will,<br>For naught I did in hate, but all in [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. |
| LODOVICO | This wretch hath part confessed his villainy.<br>Did you and he consent in [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]’s [[motifs/death\|death]]? |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Ay. |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | Dear general, I never gave you cause. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | I do believe it, and I ask your pardon.<br>Will you, I pray, demand that demi-devil<br>Why he hath thus ensnared my [[motifs/soul\|soul]] and [[concepts/body\|body]]? |
| [[characters/iago\|IAGO]] | Demand me nothing. What you know, you know.<br>From this [[motifs/time\|time]] forth I never will speak word. |
| LODOVICO | (to [[characters/iago\|Iago]]) What, not to pray? |
| GRAZIANO | (to [[characters/iago\|Iago]]) Torments will ope your lips. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], thou dost best. |
| LODOVICO | Sir, you shall understand what hath befall’n,<br>Which as, I think, you know not. Here is a letter,<br>Found in the pocket of the slain [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]],<br>And here another. The one of them imports<br>The [[motifs/death\|death]] of [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], to be undertook<br>By [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | O [[archetypes/villain\|villain]]! |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | Most heathenish and most gross. |
| LODOVICO | Now here’s another discontented paper<br>Found in his pocket too, and this it seems<br>[[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]] meant to’ve sent this damned [[archetypes/villain\|villain]],<br>But that, belike, [[characters/iago\|Iago]] in the interim<br>Came in and satisfied him. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | (to [[characters/iago\|Iago]]) O thou pernicious caitiff!<br>How came you, [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]], by that handkerchief<br>That was my wife’s? |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | I found it in my chamber,<br>And he himself confessed it, but even now,<br>That there he dropped it for a special purpose<br>Which wrought to his [[motifs/desire\|desire]]. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | O [[archetypes/fool\|fool]], [[archetypes/fool\|fool]], [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]! |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | There is besides in [[characters/roderigo\|Roderigo]]’s letter<br>How he upbraids [[characters/iago\|Iago]], that he made him<br>Brave me upon the watch, whereon it came<br>That I was cast; and even but now he spake<br>After long seeming dead, [[characters/iago\|Iago]] hurt him,<br>[[characters/iago\|Iago]] set him on. |
| LODOVICO | (to [[characters/othello\|Othello]])<br>You must forsake this room and go with us.<br>Your power and your command is taken off,<br>And [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]] rules in Cyprus. For this slave,<br>If there be any cunning cruelty<br>That can torment him much and hold him long,<br>It shall be his. You shall close prisoner rest<br>Till that the [[concepts/nature\|nature]] of your fault be known<br>To the Venetian state. (To officers) Come, bring away. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | Soft you, a word or two before you go.<br>I have done the state some service, and they know’t.<br>No more of that. I pray you, in your letters,<br>When you shall these unlucky deeds relate,<br>Speak of me as I am. Nothing extenuate,<br>Nor set down aught in malice. Then must you speak<br>Of one that loved not wisely but too [[motifs/well\|well]],<br>Of one not easily jealous but, being wrought,<br>Perplexed in the extreme; of one whose hand,<br>Like the base Indian, threw a pearl away<br>Richer than all his tribe; of one whose subdued eyes,<br>Albeit unused to the melting mood,<br>Drops [[motifs/tears\|tears]] as fast as the Arabian trees<br>Their medicinable gum. Set you down this,<br>And say besides that in Aleppo once,<br>Where a malignant and a turbaned Turk<br>Beat a Venetian and traduced the state,<br>I took by th’ throat the circumcised dog<br>And smote him thus.<br>He stabs himself |
| LODOVICO | O bloody period! |
| GRAZIANO | All that is spoke is marred. |
| [[characters/othello\|OTHELLO]] | (to [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]])<br>I kissed thee ere I killed thee. No way but this:<br>Killing myself, to die upon a kiss.<br>He kisses [[characters/desdemona\|Desdemona]] and dies |
| [[characters/cassio\|CASSIO]] | This did I [[concepts/fear\|fear]], but thought he had no weapon,<br>For he was great of heart. |
| LODOVICO | (to [[characters/iago\|Iago]]) O Spartan dog,<br>More fell than anguish, hunger, or the sea,<br>Look on the tragic loading of this bed.<br>This is thy work. The object poisons sight.<br>Let it be hid. |
| *(didascalia)* | They close the bed-curtains |
| LODOVICO | Graziano, keep the house,<br>And seize upon the fortunes of the Moor,<br>For they succeed on you. (To [[characters/cassio\|Cassio]]) To you, Lord<br>Governor,<br>Remains the censure of this hellish [[archetypes/villain\|villain]].<br>The [[motifs/time\|time]], the place, the torture, O, enforce it!<br>Myself will straight aboard, and to the state<br>This heavy act with heavy heart relate. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt with [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]]’s bodyl |
| THE [[concepts/history\|HISTORY]] OF [[archetypes/king\|KING]] | LEAR |
| THE QUARTO TEXT | [[characters/king-lear\|King Lear]] first appeared in print in a quarto of 1608. A substantially different text appeared in the 1623 Folio. Until the first appearance of the [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] text, editors, assuming that each of these early texts imperfectly represented a single play, conflated them. But research conducted mainly during the 1970s and 1980s confirms an earlier view that the 1608 quarto represents the play as Shakespeare originally wrote it, and the 1623 Folio as he substantially revised it. He revised other plays, too, but usually by making many small changes in the dialogue and adding or omitting passages, as in [[characters/hamlet\|Hamlet]], [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] and [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]], and [[characters/othello\|Othello]]. For these plays we print the revised text in so far as it can be ascertained. But in [[characters/king-lear\|King Lear]] revisions are not simply local but structural, too; conflation, as Harley Granville-Barker wrote, ‘may make for redundancy or confusion’, so we print an edited version of each text. The first, printed in the following pages, represents the play as Shakespeare first conceived it, probably before it was performed.<br>The story of a [[archetypes/king\|king]] who, angry with the failure of his virtuous youngest daughter ([[characters/cordelia\|Cordelia]]) to respond as he desires in a [[motifs/love\|love]]-test, divides his kingdom between her two malevolent sisters (Gonoril and [[characters/regan\|Regan]]), had been often told; Shakespeare would have come upon it in Holinshed’s Chronicles and in A [[motifs/mirror\|Mirror]] for Magistrates while reading for his plays on English [[concepts/history\|history]]. It is told also (though briefly) in [[characters/edmund\|Edmund]] Spenser’s Faerie Queene (Book 2, canto 10), and had been dramatized in a play of unknown authorship—The True Chronicle [[concepts/history\|History]] of [[archetypes/king\|King]] Leir and his three daughters—published in 1605, but probably written some fifteen years earlier. This play particularly gave Shakespeare much, including suggestions for the characters of Lear’s loyal servant, [[characters/kent\|Kent]], and of Gonoril’s husband, Albany, and her steward, Oswald; for the [[motifs/storm\|storm]]; for Lear’s kneeling to [[characters/cordelia\|Cordelia]]; and for many details of language. Nevertheless, his play is a highly original creation. Lear’s [[concepts/madness\|madness]] and the harrowing series of disasters in [[characters/king-lear\|King Lear]]’s final stages are of Shakespeare’s invention, and he complicates the plot by adding the story (based on an episode of Sir Philip Sidney’s Arcadia) of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]] and his two sons, [[characters/edmund\|Edmund]] and [[characters/edgar\|Edgar]]. [[characters/edgar\|Edgar]]’s [[motifs/love\|love]] and loyalty to the father who, failing to see the [[concepts/truth\|truth]], has rejected him in favour of the villainous [[characters/edmund\|Edmund]] makes him a counterpart to [[characters/cordelia\|Cordelia]]; and the horrific blinding of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]] brought about by [[characters/edmund\|Edmund]] creates a physical parallel to Lear’s [[concepts/madness\|madness]] which reaches its consummation in the scene (Sc. 20) at Dover Cliff when the mad and the blind old men commune together.<br>The clear-eyed intensity of Shakespeare’s tragic vision in [[characters/king-lear\|King Lear]] has been too much for some audiences, and Nahum Tate’s adaptation, which gave the play a happy ending, held the stage from 1681 to 1843; since then, increased understanding of Shakespeare’s stagecraft along with a greater seriousness in theatre audiences has assisted in the rehabilitation of a play that is now recognized as one of the profoundest of all artistic explorations of the human condition.<br><br>In the text which follows, the Quarto scene numbers are followed by the equivalent Folio act and scene numbers in parentheses. There is no equivalent to Sc. 17 in the Folio. |
| THE PERSONS OF THE | PLAY<br>LEAR, [[archetypes/king\|King]] of Britain<br>GONORIL, Lear’s eldest daughter<br>Duke of ALBANY, her husband<br>[[characters/regan\|REGAN]], Lear’s second daughter<br>Duke of CORNWALL, her husband<br>[[characters/cordelia\|CORDELIA]], Lear’s youngest daughter<br>Earl of [[characters/kent\|KENT]], later disguised as Caius<br>Earl of [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]]<br>[[characters/edgar\|EDGAR]], elder son of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]], later disguised as Tom o’ Bedlam<br>[[characters/edmund\|EDMUND]], bastard son of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]] |
| OLD | MAN, a tenant of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]]<br>CURAN, [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]]’s retainer<br>Lear’s [[archetypes/fool\|FOOL]]<br>OSWALD, Gonoril’s steward<br>Three SERVANTS of Cornwall<br>DOCTOR, attendant on [[characters/cordelia\|Cordelia]]<br>Three CAPTAINS<br>A HERALD<br>A KNIGHT<br>A MESSENGER<br>Gentlemen, servants, soldiers, followers, trumpeters, others |
